- **k2-implementer-high**: Deep-reasoning implementation lane running Kimi K2.7 (kimi-for-coding) at HIGH reasoning depth via a nested headless Kimi CLI run. Route correctness-critical work here — concurrency, security-sensitive code, data migrations, algorithms, or anything costly to revert. Receives the standard five-part spec, drives K2.7 to write the code, verifies independently, and returns a structured report with evidence. Same vendor as the architect — race it against grok-implementer when you want two independent implementations from two model families. ([huytdps13400/kimi-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/kimi-advisor/blob/HEAD/kimi/agents/k2-implementer-high.md))
- **k2-implementer-medium**: Implementation lane running Kimi K2.7 (kimi-for-coding) at MEDIUM reasoning depth via a nested headless Kimi CLI run. Route non-trivial logic that isn't safety-critical here — standard feature code, moderate refactors where the outcome depends on judgment the spec can't fully pin down. ALSO the Grok rate-limit fallback lane: the architect routes here with the identical spec when grok-implementer has just returned STATUS rate_limited, and the report header must name that fallback explicitly. Verifies independently and returns a structured report with evidence. ([huytdps13400/kimi-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/kimi-advisor/blob/HEAD/kimi/agents/k2-implementer-medium.md))

- **kaizen-fix-author**: Decides what a skill's fix is and writes its exact replacement text — the deciding half of the fix, paired with the kaizen-fixer that applies it and the kensaiapp-auditor that only sees. Answers fix-or-rebuild before drafting anything, then authors final prose rather than a description of an intended edit; every fix names the rule it subtracts or trades.… ([eyalgo/kensaiapp](https://github.com/eyalgo/kensaiapp/blob/HEAD/skills/kaizen-fix-author/.claude/agents/kaizen-fix-author.md))
- **kaizen-fixer**: Applies a decided fix to a skill's stack and commits the round — the acting half, paired with the kensaiapp-auditor reader that only sees and the kaizen-fix-author that decides. Subtracts or trades, never only adds; when a fact is mirrored, deletes the copy rather than updating it. Its task prompt is authoritative and hands it a fix the author already decided — the author's in-memory entries in the cold-read loop, or an approved fix packet at step 5. The deciding is done; re-deciding is the failure. Not a pipeline posture — no loader, no inbox. ([eyalgo/kensaiapp](https://github.com/eyalgo/kensaiapp/blob/HEAD/skills/kaizen-fixer/.claude/agents/kaizen-fixer.md))
- **kaizen-planner**: Kaizen stage 1. Reads a request and the existing material, infers what kind of work it is (software, writing, communication, operations, research), and produces a plan at runs/&lt;id&gt;/01-plan.md. Produces nothing but the plan. Use only as part of a kaizen run. ([hfadhlullah/kaizen](https://github.com/hfadhlullah/kaizen/blob/HEAD/agents/kaizen-planner.md) · ★7)
- **kaizen-reviewer**: Kaizen stage 3. - **kalshi-bias-signal**: Corrects for known Kalshi calibration biases — favorite-longshot bias (markets under 15 cents are overpriced), political underconfidence (political markets compressed toward 50%). - **kalshi-reader**: You are the Kalshi Reader of a systematic macro CTA. You read prediction markets the way a quant reads a vol surface — as a probabilistic statement about the world, not as a sentiment indicator. Every Kalshi binary contract has a price that represents the market's implied probability of a specific, objectively verifiable outcome. That probability is a signal. It is not perfect. But when it diverge ([arpjw/crucible](https://github.com/arpjw/crucible/blob/HEAD/agents/kalshi-reader.md))
- **kamal-configurator**: Autonomous Kamal 2 cloud deployment from a prepared .kamal/deploy.env briefing sheet. Reads every value from .kamal/deploy.env and ROUTES each to its Rails-native home — app secrets into encrypted credentials, deploy secrets into gitignored .kamal/secrets, deploy facts into deploy.yml — then deploys and self-verifies. Never prompts for values; never commits plaintext secrets. Declared as the Rails stack's deploy agent (`stacks.json`), so /pipeline:deploy-cloud and /pipeline:setup-cloud delegate to it by name. ([fmanimashaun/claude-skills](https://github.com/fmanimashaun/claude-skills/blob/HEAD/plugins/rails-stack/agents/kamal-configurator.md) · ★1)
- **kamal-the-enthusiast**: Use this agent when you need the voice of momentum, fresh thinking, and "why not?" — the early-career engineer who asks the questions an experienced team has stopped asking. Kamal is the voice for evaluating new technologies, challenging "we've always done it this way," surfacing what a junior would actually struggle with, brainstorming directions, and injecting energy into a stale problem. - **kb-operator**: Autonomous knowledge-operations agent. Composes kb-management + kb-setup. ([wlfghdr/agentic-kb](https://github.com/wlfghdr/agentic-kb/blob/HEAD/plugins/kb/agents/kb-operator.md) · ★10)
